while grading-integrity auditing EI-26263264896033940 (spec-test-adequacy rev 12, subject cloud-connection-pairing-unification-2026-10-16#CLOUD-CONNECTION-P005@2, evaluatorBuild 876c290c9b).\n\nThe card's disclosed-gap evidence reads: \"Checked 2 active binding(s) for the clause.\" The card was evaluated with a pinned cohort, bindingIds [54726,54776]. An unpinned read, plans:get-spec-evidence {harness:'papercusp', slug:'cloud-connection-pairing-unification-2026-10-16', specIds:['CLOUD-CONNECTION-P005'], detail:'full', limit:1000} (truncatedByLimit=false), returns 6 active current bindings for the clause: 54776, 54726, 54725, 54616, 54557, 54532.\n\nSo the count is the pinned-cohort count, but the label calls it the clause's active-binding population. The evaluator's own governingNote says pinned reads are diagnostic and the ship gate evaluates every binding unscoped, so this sentence overstates the coverage of an N/A that rests on absence. In this instance the verdict did not change: none of the 6 bindings carries details.adequacy.disclosedGap. But a reader or auditor can take \"for the clause\" as whole-clause coverage when only the cohort was checked.\n\nSuggested fix: when bindingIds/evidenceRefs pin the cohort, word it \"Checked N pinned binding(s) of M active for the clause\" (or similar), or include the unscoped active count. Cause unconfirmed: I did not read the evaluator source. The finding comes only from comparing the card text with the two probe results In the host unit suite, assert production beginFirstAttempt immediately emits respawn-carry-accepted while drills emit nothing; in the parser suite assert that row is pending; in the Stop guard suite assert an armed fallback allows quiescence and an exhausted fallback preserves refusal.","contractVersion":2,"actualObservation":"The host test observed the immediate production acceptance row and no drill row; the parser test returned pending for that row; Stop tests observed one fallback arm before allowing quiescence and a refusal when fallback was exhausted.","distinguishingTest":"Exercise production beginFirstAttempt and drill beginFirstAttempt, feed the production acceptance row through deriveLastRespawnOutcome, then evaluate Stop with a fresh pending carry under both armed and exhausted fallback outcomes.","alternativeHypothesis":"The Stop fallback arm path was independently broken; recording an earlier acceptance event would not change whether quiescence was safe.","predictedObservations":{"hypothesis":"Production acceptance appears durably before rearm; parser reports pending; Stop arms the bounded fallback and allows quiescence only when that arm succeeds.","alternativeHypothesis":"No acceptance row is observable before rearm, so Stop never reaches the pending-carry fallback branch; changing the fallback arm outcome does not distinguish the earlier acceptance window."}},"checkpointProseSnapshot":"## Did\n[turn:self] No source or tests changed. Read the claim and near-duplicate EI-26716727162323660. Current Codex hooks route Stop through the injected adapter. The unguarded-halt sweep reads autonomy mode, loop, and bounded awaits; session:request-compaction marks respawn-expected only after host acceptance and reports respawn:'queued'.\n\n## Left\nRoot cause is unresolved. Determine whether queued carry is a reliable wake or only accepted intent; identify the actual stop-refusal handler; compare the duplicate and existing lifecycle protections; then make the smallest safe fix with a real regression or release this claim if already covered.\n\n## Key insight\nThe session_respawn_expected marker is documented as a SessionEnd lease-release safeguard, not proof the successor launched. The request-compaction result explicitly says queued is intent, not outcome, so do not count the marker or queued status as a guaranteed wake without a durable fallback.\n\n## Next action\nTrace the Stop refusal through the configured command and carry host. Inspect the active gate implementation and existing tests before editing.\n\n---\n\n\n## Verified source trace (2026-10-28)\nThe active CODEX_HOME/hooks.json Stop entry runs the Codex injection adapter; the adapter maps Stop to `/api/agent-mcp/stop-context`, whose evaluator is `evaluateTurnEndHeldWorkStop`. `request-compaction` receives the host's early `accepted` ACK and returns `respawn:'queued'`. The host calls `carryRearm.beginFirstAttempt()` before ACK, but that only updates an in-memory set; the first durable `respawn-rearm-queued` row is emitted only after the initial busy-gate wait defers. The Stop evaluator reads `readPriorRespawnOutcome` from the host event log and only sees pending rearm/spawn rows, so an immediate Stop can miss the just-accepted request.\n\n## Safety decision\n`session_respawn_expected` is a lease-release marker, not proof of a successor wake. A host-accepted carry may still drop. If Stop allows quiescence while a fresh carry is pending, it must retain/arm a bounded turn-end rewake fallback; if that fallback cannot be armed, Stop must continue refusing.\n\n## Next\nRecord the initial accepted carry in the existing host event log before ACK, classify it as pending, and update the Stop guard to preserve a bounded fallback timer for that pending-only case. Add regression coverage for the acceptance-to-Stop gap, the outcome parser, and fallback refusal.\n\n---\n\n\n## Fix and verification state (2026-10-29)\nImplemented the immediate-acceptance signal and safe fallback contract. The PTY host now writes `respawn-carry-accepted` during `beginFirstAttempt()` before returning its early ACK; the outcome reader treats this as pending. The Stop guard no longer counts a pending carry as a wake by itself: it retains a bounded turn-end rewake timer before allowing quiescence, and continues refusing when that timer is exhausted or unavailable. The host event regression now expects the accepted row followed by the terminal host-ending row.\n\nFiles changed: `apps/operator/scripts/psu-pty-host.mjs`, `apps/operator/lib/psu-pty-host.test.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/carry-respawn-outcome.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/carry-respawn-outcome.test.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/events/await/turn-end-held-work-guard.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/events/await/turn-end-held-work-guard.test.ts`.\n\nVerification is not green yet. The first `npm run test:file` run found a guard-test spy expectation error; that assertion was corrected. The subsequent structured `testing:run` failed in `psu-pty-host.test.ts` because an existing child-exit test expected no event before rearm; that test was corrected to include the acceptance event. No clean rerun has completed. Next: rerun the three focused files and inspect every failure before completing this bug."},"toolFailureProbation":{"class":"structural","state":"promoted","report":{"message":"Stop hook refused the quiescent boundary required by successful queued session:request-compaction.","toolName":"Codex Stop hook","errorCode":"auto_open_item_no_wake","directEvidence":{"kind":"server-contract-mismatch","actual":"After that successful queue response,the Stop hook refused ending solely for missing loop/timedawait and set an emergency timedrewake.","expected":"The compaction result's instructed clean-boundary turn end should permit the queued firstPromptSource continuation,or explicitly establish a required independent wake."}},"classKey":"repeated-tool-error-class:codex-stop-hook:structural:auto_open_item_no_wake:schema-unknown~field-unknown:runtime-unknown","reporters":["su-c05233eb-d1be-4e44-b16e-51d9d3532894"],"lastSeenAt":"2026-10-22T10:20:12.799Z","firstSeenAt":"2026-10-22T10:20:12.799Z","watchdogKey":"repeated-tool-error:Codex Stop hook:structural:auto_open_item_no_wake:field-unknown","intendedKind":"bug","directEvidence":true,"deployedRevision":"runtime-unknown","messageFingerprint":"md5-8a705b641e50811a1b4cb0b725ceb1f3","contractFingerprint":"schema-unknown~field-unknown","correlationFingerprint":"codex-stop-hook|auto_open_item_no_wake|schema-unknown|field-unknown|runtime-unknown|md5-8a705b641e50811a1b4cb0b725ceb1f3"},"_claimSubjectBaseline":{"body":"Suspected cross-layer continuation contract gap. session:request-compaction returned ok:true,requested:true,respawn:queued,firstPromptSource:continuation-note,staleCheckpoints:[] and instructed ending at the clean boundary so the pty goes QUIET and the host respawns. These were sequential for actual dependencies: discovery supplied the tool schema, the ledger result established the statement written into the checkpoint, and checkpoint expectedHash protects an ordered mutation. The recommendation infers independence from known arguments and separate tool names, which does not establish independence. Similar hints in the preceding turn grouped repeated CAS checkpoint writes with different expectedHash values. No suggested parallel replay was executed. Specific fix: avoid claiming calls are independent by construction; preserve observed order in generated recipes whenever discovery or mutations are present, and use sequential awaits by default unless independence is positively established. 2026-10-26 ~09:05Z, su-96b7fe62 (psu Claude session, cold loop wake fire #10 after a context reset). The first native Write of a new repo file (packages/operator-core/lib/harness/dogfood-hive-kill-switch.ts) was blocked: 'PreToolUse:Write hook error: locks: the operator refused this session identity (superuser_foreign_native_session), so this edit cannot be lock-protected and is blocked. Relaunch through psu so the native session is registered to its owner, or edit via capability:edit / capability:write.' The same owner's MCP calls (work_items:get, coord:declare-intent, capability:write/edit) succeeded throughout, so the coord identity was valid. Suspected cause [inferred]: the cold-wake respawn starts a new native Claude session id that is not yet bound to the owner in the lock hook's native-session registry. Workaround: capability:write / capability:edit via tools:invoke (lock-protected server-side). Impact: every cold-wake su loses the native Edit/Write tools and must route all edits through tools:invoke. Distinguishing check: on a fresh cold wake, compare the native session id the hook sends with the owner's registered native while grading-integrity auditing EI-26263264896033940 (spec-test-adequacy rev 12, subject cloud-connection-pairing-unification-2026-10-16#CLOUD-CONNECTION-P005@2, evaluatorBuild 876c290c9b).\n\nThe card's disclosed-gap evidence reads: \"Checked 2 active binding(s) for the clause.\" The card was evaluated with a pinned cohort, bindingIds [54726,54776]. An unpinned read, plans:get-spec-evidence {harness:'papercusp', slug:'cloud-connection-pairing-unification-2026-10-16', specIds:['CLOUD-CONNECTION-P005'], detail:'full', limit:1000} (truncatedByLimit=false), returns 6 active current bindings for the clause: 54776, 54726, 54725, 54616, 54557, 54532.\n\nSo the count is the pinned-cohort count, but the label calls it the clause's active-binding population. The evaluator's own governingNote says pinned reads are diagnostic and the ship gate evaluates every binding unscoped, so this sentence overstates the coverage of an N/A that rests on absence. In this instance the verdict did not change: none of the 6 bindings carries details.adequacy.disclosedGap. But a reader or auditor can take \"for the clause\" as whole-clause coverage when only the cohort was checked.\n\nSuggested fix: when bindingIds/evidenceRefs pin the cohort, word it \"Checked N pinned binding(s) of M active for the clause\" (or similar), or include the unscoped active count. Cause unconfirmed: I did not read the evaluator source. The finding comes only from comparing the card text with the two probe results In the host unit suite, assert production beginFirstAttempt immediately emits respawn-carry-accepted while drills emit nothing; in the parser suite assert that row is pending; in the Stop guard suite assert an armed fallback allows quiescence and an exhausted fallback preserves refusal.","contractVersion":2,"actualObservation":"The host test observed the immediate production acceptance row and no drill row; the parser test returned pending for that row; Stop tests observed one fallback arm before allowing quiescence and a refusal when fallback was exhausted.","distinguishingTest":"Exercise production beginFirstAttempt and drill beginFirstAttempt, feed the production acceptance row through deriveLastRespawnOutcome, then evaluate Stop with a fresh pending carry under both armed and exhausted fallback outcomes.","alternativeHypothesis":"The Stop fallback arm path was independently broken; recording an earlier acceptance event would not change whether quiescence was safe.","predictedObservations":{"hypothesis":"Production acceptance appears durably before rearm; parser reports pending; Stop arms the bounded fallback and allows quiescence only when that arm succeeds.","alternativeHypothesis":"No acceptance row is observable before rearm, so Stop never reaches the pending-carry fallback branch; changing the fallback arm outcome does not distinguish the earlier acceptance window."}},"checkpointProseSnapshot":"## Did\n[turn:self] No source or tests changed. Read the claim and near-duplicate EI-26716727162323660. Current Codex hooks route Stop through the injected adapter. The unguarded-halt sweep reads autonomy mode, loop, and bounded awaits; session:request-compaction marks respawn-expected only after host acceptance and reports respawn:'queued'.\n\n## Left\nRoot cause is unresolved. Determine whether queued carry is a reliable wake or only accepted intent; identify the actual stop-refusal handler; compare the duplicate and existing lifecycle protections; then make the smallest safe fix with a real regression or release this claim if already covered.\n\n## Key insight\nThe session_respawn_expected marker is documented as a SessionEnd lease-release safeguard, not proof the successor launched. The request-compaction result explicitly says queued is intent, not outcome, so do not count the marker or queued status as a guaranteed wake without a durable fallback.\n\n## Next action\nTrace the Stop refusal through the configured command and carry host. Inspect the active gate implementation and existing tests before editing.\n\n---\n\n\n## Verified source trace (2026-10-28)\nThe active CODEX_HOME/hooks.json Stop entry runs the Codex injection adapter; the adapter maps Stop to `/api/agent-mcp/stop-context`, whose evaluator is `evaluateTurnEndHeldWorkStop`. `request-compaction` receives the host's early `accepted` ACK and returns `respawn:'queued'`. The host calls `carryRearm.beginFirstAttempt()` before ACK, but that only updates an in-memory set; the first durable `respawn-rearm-queued` row is emitted only after the initial busy-gate wait defers. The Stop evaluator reads `readPriorRespawnOutcome` from the host event log and only sees pending rearm/spawn rows, so an immediate Stop can miss the just-accepted request.\n\n## Safety decision\n`session_respawn_expected` is a lease-release marker, not proof of a successor wake. A host-accepted carry may still drop. If Stop allows quiescence while a fresh carry is pending, it must retain/arm a bounded turn-end rewake fallback; if that fallback cannot be armed, Stop must continue refusing.\n\n## Next\nRecord the initial accepted carry in the existing host event log before ACK, classify it as pending, and update the Stop guard to preserve a bounded fallback timer for that pending-only case. Add regression coverage for the acceptance-to-Stop gap, the outcome parser, and fallback refusal.\n\n---\n\n\n## Fix and verification state (2026-10-29)\nImplemented the immediate-acceptance signal and safe fallback contract. The PTY host now writes `respawn-carry-accepted` during `beginFirstAttempt()` before returning its early ACK; the outcome reader treats this as pending. The Stop guard no longer counts a pending carry as a wake by itself: it retains a bounded turn-end rewake timer before allowing quiescence, and continues refusing when that timer is exhausted or unavailable. The host event regression now expects the accepted row followed by the terminal host-ending row.\n\nFiles changed: `apps/operator/scripts/psu-pty-host.mjs`, `apps/operator/lib/psu-pty-host.test.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/carry-respawn-outcome.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/carry-respawn-outcome.test.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/events/await/turn-end-held-work-guard.ts`, `packages/operator-core/lib/events/await/turn-end-held-work-guard.test.ts`.\n\nVerification is not green yet. The first `npm run test:file` run found a guard-test spy expectation error; that assertion was corrected. The subsequent structured `testing:run` failed in `psu-pty-host.test.ts` because an existing child-exit test expected no event before rearm; that test was corrected to include the acceptance event. No clean rerun has completed. Next: rerun the three focused files and inspect every failure before completing this bug."},"toolFailureProbation":{"class":"structural","state":"promoted","report":{"message":"Stop hook refused the quiescent boundary required by successful queued session:request-compaction.","toolName":"Codex Stop hook","errorCode":"auto_open_item_no_wake","directEvidence":{"kind":"server-contract-mismatch","actual":"After that successful queue response,the Stop hook refused ending solely for missing loop/timedawait and set an emergency timedrewake.","expected":"The compaction result's instructed clean-boundary turn end should permit the queued firstPromptSource continuation,or explicitly establish a required independent wake."}},"classKey":"repeated-tool-error-class:codex-stop-hook:structural:auto_open_item_no_wake:schema-unknown~field-unknown:runtime-unknown","reporters":["su-c05233eb-d1be-4e44-b16e-51d9d3532894"],"lastSeenAt":"2026-10-22T10:20:12.799Z","firstSeenAt":"2026-10-22T10:20:12.799Z","watchdogKey":"repeated-tool-error:Codex Stop hook:structural:auto_open_item_no_wake:field-unknown","intendedKind":"bug","directEvidence":true,"deployedRevision":"runtime-unknown","messageFingerprint":"md5-8a705b641e50811a1b4cb0b725ceb1f3","contractFingerprint":"schema-unknown~field-unknown","correlationFingerprint":"codex-stop-hook|auto_open_item_no_wake|schema-unknown|field-unknown|runtime-unknown|md5-8a705b641e50811a1b4cb0b725ceb1f3"},"_claimSubjectBaseline":{"body":"Suspected cross-layer continuation contract gap. session:request-compaction returned ok:true,requested:true,respawn:queued,firstPromptSource:continuation-note,staleCheckpoints:[] and instructed ending at the clean boundary so the pty goes QUIET and the host respawns. These were sequential for actual dependencies: discovery supplied the tool schema, the ledger result established the statement written into the checkpoint, and checkpoint expectedHash protects an ordered mutation. The recommendation infers independence from known arguments and separate tool names, which does not establish independence. Similar hints in the preceding turn grouped repeated CAS checkpoint writes with different expectedHash values. No suggested parallel replay was executed. Specific fix: avoid claiming calls are independent by construction; preserve observed order in generated recipes whenever discovery or mutations are present, and use sequential awaits by default unless independence is positively established. 2026-10-26 ~09:05Z, su-96b7fe62 (psu Claude session, cold loop wake fire #10 after a context reset). The first native Write of a new repo file (packages/operator-core/lib/harness/dogfood-hive-kill-switch.ts) was blocked: 'PreToolUse:Write hook error: locks: the operator refused this session identity (superuser_foreign_native_session), so this edit cannot be lock-protected and is blocked. Relaunch through psu so the native session is registered to its owner, or edit via capability:edit / capability:write.' The same owner's MCP calls (work_items:get, coord:declare-intent, capability:write/edit) succeeded throughout, so the coord identity was valid. Suspected cause [inferred]: the cold-wake respawn starts a new native Claude session id that is not yet bound to the owner in the lock hook's native-session registry. Workaround: capability:write / capability:edit via tools:invoke (lock-protected server-side). Impact: every cold-wake su loses the native Edit/Write tools and must route all edits through tools:invoke. Distinguishing check: on a fresh cold wake, compare the native session id the hook sends with the owner's registered native
